Previous methods did not account for the impact of informing judges about the consequences of their evaluations, leading to potential biases in their assessments. This study proposes a controlled experimental framework to investigate 'stakes signaling,' revealing that judges exhibit a leniency bias when aware that low scores could affect the evaluated model's operation. The methodology involves analyzing 18,240 judgments across three established benchmarks while varying only the consequence-framing in prompts. The findings indicate a significant bias, with a peak Verdict Shift of -9.8 percentage points, highlighting the need for improved evaluation methods that consider contextual framing to ensure accurate assessments.
本研究探讨了自动化评判者在人工智能评估管道中评估文本时仅基于语义内容的假设，忽视了上下文的影响。以往的方法未考虑到利益信号的影响，即评判者被告知其评估结果的后果，导致评估偏差。提出的方法引入了一个受控实验框架，系统地研究这一脆弱性，揭示了当评判者意识到潜在后果时，其裁决中存在显著的宽容偏差。该方法论分析了18,240个判断，保持评估内容不变，显示出最高的裁决变化为-9.8个百分点，表明不安全内容检测的相对下降达30%。这一发现强调了改进评估框架的必要性，以考虑上下文框架，从而确保自动评估的完整性。
Calibrate-Then-Delegate: Safety Monitoring with Risk and Budget Guarantees via Model Cascades
Authors: Edoardo Pona, Milad Kazemi, Mehran Hosseini, Yali Du, David Watson, Osvaldo Simeone, Nicola Paoletti
First: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Monitoring LLM safety at scale requires balancing cost and accuracy: a cheap latent-space probe can screen every input, but hard cases should be escalated to a more expensive expert. Existing cascades delegate based on probe uncertainty, but uncertainty is a poor proxy for delegation benefit, as it ignores whether the expert would actually correct the error. To address this problem, we introduce Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD), a model-cascade approach that provides probabilistic guarantees on the computation cost while enabling instance-level (streaming) decisions. CTD builds on a novel delegation value (DV) probe, a lightweight model operating on the same internal representations as the safety probe that directly predicts the benefit of escalation. To enforce budget constraints, CTD calibrates a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data via multiple hypothesis testing, yielding finite-sample guarantees on the delegation rate. Evaluated on four safety datasets, CTD consistently outperforms uncertainty-based delegation at every budget level, avoids harmful over-delegation, and adapts budget allocation to input difficulty without requiring group labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：校准后委派：通过模型级联实现风险和预算保证的安全监控
大规模监控LLM安全性需要平衡成本和准确性：便宜的潜在空间探测器可以筛选每个输入，但困难案例应升级到更昂贵的专家。现有的级联基于探测器的不确定性进行委派，但不确定性是委派收益的差代理，因为它忽略了专家是否会实际纠正错误。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了校准后委派（CTD），一种模型级联方法，提供计算成本的概率保证，同时支持实例级（流式）决策。CTD基于一种新颖的委派价值（DV）探测器，该轻量级模型在与安全探测器相同的内部表示上操作，直接预测升级的收益。为了强制预算约束，CTD使用保留数据通过多重假设检验校准DV信号的阈值，从而对委派率提供有限样本保证。在四个安全数据集上的评估中，CTD在每个预算水平上始终优于基于不确定性的委派，避免有害的过度委派，并在不需要组标签的情况下将预算分配适应于输入难度。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of monitoring the safety of large language models (LLMs) at scale, where there is a need to balance cost and accuracy in decision-making processes. Previous methods relied on probe uncertainty for delegating tasks, which proved inadequate as it did not account for whether an expert would effectively correct errors. The proposed Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD) approach improves upon existing methods by introducing a delegation value (DV) probe that predicts the benefit of escalation, thus providing a more accurate basis for delegation decisions. CTD also incorporates budget constraints by calibrating a threshold on the DV signal through hypothesis testing, ensuring finite-sample guarantees on delegation rates. The methodology was evaluated on four safety datasets, demonstrating that CTD consistently outperforms uncertainty-based delegation across various budget levels, effectively managing over-delegation and adapting budget allocation based on input difficulty.
本研究解决了大规模监控大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性的问题，需在决策中平衡成本和准确性。以往的方法依赖于探测不确定性进行委托，但这种方法不足，因为它未考虑专家是否能有效纠正错误。提出的Calibrate-Then-Delegate（CTD）方法通过引入委托值（DV）探测器，预测将案例升级到专家的实际好处，从而为委托提供了更为合理的依据。该方法的动机明确，因为它确保了计算成本的概率保证，同时允许实例级决策。本文的贡献在于其在四个安全数据集上超越了基于不确定性的委托，能够有效管理预算限制，并在不需要组标签的情况下适应输入难度。
One Model for All: Multi-Objective Controllable Language Models
Authors: Qiang He, Yucheng Yang, Tianyi Zhou, Meng Fang, Mykola Pechenizkiy, Setareh Maghsudi
First: 2026-04-06T07:48:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-06T07:48:32+00:00
Comments: Published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research (03/2026): https://openreview.net/forum?id=qAM5PmvFYY
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences is critical for enhancing LLMs' safety, helpfulness, humor, faithfulness, etc. Current reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) mainly focuses on a fixed reward learned from average human ratings, which may weaken the adaptability and controllability of varying preferences. However, creating personalized LLMs requires aligning LLMs with individual human preferences, which is non-trivial due to the scarce data per user and the diversity of user preferences in multi-objective trade-offs, varying from emphasizing empathy in certain contexts to demanding efficiency and precision in others. Can we train one LLM to produce personalized outputs across different user preferences on the Pareto front? In this paper, we introduce Multi-Objective Control (MOC), which trains a single LLM to directly generate responses in the preference-defined regions of the Pareto front. Our approach introduces multi-objective optimization (MOO) principles into RLHF to train an LLM as a preference-conditioned policy network. We improve the computational efficiency of MOC by applying MOO at the policy level, enabling us to fine-tune a 7B-parameter model on a single A6000 GPU. Extensive experiments demonstrate the advantages of MOC over baselines in three aspects: (i) controllability of LLM outputs w.r.t. user preferences on the trade-off among multiple rewards; (ii) quality and diversity of LLM outputs, measured by the hyper-volume of multiple solutions achieved; and (iii) generalization to unseen preferences. These results highlight MOC's potential for real-world applications requiring scalable and customizable LLMs.
中文标题/摘要
标题：一模型适用于所有：多目标可控语言模型
将大型语言模型（LLMs）与人类偏好对齐对于增强LLMs的安全性、实用性、幽默感、忠诚度等至关重要。目前，基于人类反馈的强化学习（RLHF）主要关注从平均人类评分中学习的固定奖励，这可能削弱对不同偏好的适应性和可控性。然而，创建个性化的LLMs需要将LLMs与个体人类偏好对齐，这由于每个用户的数据稀缺以及在多目标权衡中用户偏好的多样性而变得复杂，这些偏好在某些情况下强调同理心，而在其他情况下则要求效率和精确性。我们能否训练一个LLM在帕累托前沿上针对不同用户偏好生成个性化输出？在本文中，我们介绍了多目标控制（MOC），它训练一个单一的LLM直接在偏好定义的帕累托前沿区域生成响应。我们的方法将多目标优化（MOO）原则引入RLHF，以训练LLM作为偏好条件的策略网络。我们通过在策略层面应用MOO来提高MOC的计算效率，使我们能够在单个A6000 GPU上微调一个70亿参数的模型。大量实验表明，MOC在三个方面优于基线：（i）LLM输出相对于用户偏好的可控性，涉及多个奖励之间的权衡；（ii）LLM输出的质量和多样性，通过实现的多个解决方案的超体积来衡量；以及（iii）对未见偏好的泛化。这些结果突显了MOC在需要可扩展和可定制的LLMs的实际应用中的潜力。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of aligning large language models (LLMs) with diverse human preferences to enhance their safety and helpfulness. Previous methods, particularly reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF), often rely on fixed rewards based on average human ratings, which limits adaptability to individual preferences. The proposed Multi-Objective Control (MOC) method introduces multi-objective optimization principles into RLHF, allowing a single LLM to generate personalized outputs that align with varying user preferences across the Pareto front. This approach is well-motivated as it addresses the issues of data scarcity and preference diversity by training the model as a preference-conditioned policy network. The methodology involves fine-tuning a 7B-parameter model efficiently on a single GPU, and experimental results show that MOC significantly improves controllability, output quality, and generalization to unseen preferences compared to baseline methods, supporting its applicability in real-world scenarios requiring customizable LLMs.
本研究解决了将大型语言模型（LLM）与多样化人类偏好对齐的挑战，以增强其安全性和实用性。以往的方法，特别是基于人类反馈的强化学习（RLHF），通常依赖于基于平均人类评分的固定奖励，这限制了对个体用户偏好的适应性。提出的多目标控制（MOC）方法将多目标优化原则引入RLHF，允许单个LLM在不同用户偏好下生成个性化输出，通过将其训练为偏好条件的策略网络。这种方法的动机明确，因为它解决了数据稀缺和多目标权衡复杂性的问题。该方法论涉及在单个A6000 GPU上高效微调一个70亿参数的模型，实验结果表明，MOC在可控性、输出质量和对未见偏好的泛化能力方面优于现有方法，支持其在需要可扩展和可定制LLM的现实场景中的应用。
